# Westernesse: consolidated open register

**Read as tickets and features (15 November).** `bun scripts/register-tickets.ts` places every row on a feature
(doc/architecture/features.json: what it is, the files it lives in, the specs that guard it) and says which rows are
live and how ready each is (ready, wants a decision, wants research, unverified). `--feature <id>` lists a feature's
live rows; `--touching <file>` says which features a changed file could touch and what is live there; `--unmapped`
lists rows with no feature. A new row must be placeable (its id in `rows`, or its prefix in `byPrefix`):
test/small/register-tickets fails with the row named.

**Closing a row (the sprint audit's lesson, 19 November).** A row is closed (fixed, met, closed) only with one of:
a spec that tests the row's own claim, named in the status (not a neighbour that passes either way: barrow-forewarned
passed without the forewarning); or a stated reason it cannot be checked headless and what would prove it (a CI
browser recording, a human session). Someone other than its closer reads the spec against the claim before it
counts: another agent, or the lead reading an agent's. A closure on a code read or one walk alone is `partly`.
Directions and future scope (KX, SS, PG, SR, IM and their kind) are counted apart from defects; the burn-down that
matters is the first hour's live P0 and P1 rows (`bun scripts/register-tickets.ts`), with 2–3 blind plays at 7/10 or
more, before a human playtest (the owner, 19 November).

Compiled 11 November 2026, read-only, from the repository at `/var/tmp/kyrie/wt-m20` (main plus local commits through
`33cf0777`) and the owner's checkout for the risk review. A draft for the M20 handoff. It is not a disposition. It
claims nothing beyond what the sources say.

**Sources read**
- `doc/history/missions/20/`:
  - log.md: amendments 1–8, the open queue, rows E1–E133;
  - handoff.md (draft), text-inference.md, user-notes.md, opening-brief.md;
  - opportunities.md, design-opportunities.md, amendment-6.md, opening-design.md;
  - cold/round-1..6: every report's "Top five problems", and round 6's §4–§17 in full.
- `doc/history/missions/19/`: closure.md, register.md (all 297 rows), inherited.md (145 rows; it reconciles
  M18's wishlist, which itself carried M17 and M16).
- `doc/history/missions/17/ahead.md`; M18 register (OWN-07 only).
- `doc/history/findings/`:
  - index.md: confirms M16 → M17 → M18 → M18 wishlist → M19 inherited was carried id by id;
  - gaps.md / gaps.tsv: 74 UNT rows never registered;
  - briefs.md: 109 BRF requirements.
- The 9 November reviews (stabilization, quest-intent, throughput). Their recommendations were carried as
  M19-239..283, and are cited through those rows.
- 2026-11-11-adventure-to-career.md, headless-rpg-investigation.md, capability-audit.md.
- `/home/jweissman/work/kyrie/doc/history/missions/20/architecture-risk-mediation-2026-11-11.md` (R1–R10).
- Memory notes: all m20-*, owner-adventure-to-career, mission-20, and the m19-owner-*, m18-owner-* and mission-*
  descriptions. The open, next and remaining lines were found by grep.

**Not read line by line:**
- the M16, M17 and M18 registers row by row: the findings index shows their open rows were carried into M19's
  inherited ledger;
- M18 closure.md: its "Carried" items are BRF-56..65;
- the M13–M15 reports.

**How it was deduped.**
- One row per problem, citing every source id. Ids are:
  - E = M20 log;
  - R = risk review;
  - R*n*-X = cold round *n*, player X;
  - M19-/PD- = M19 register;
  - other prefixes (ARV, REV, OWN, PT, …) = M19 inherited;
  - UNT = findings gaps;
  - BRF = briefs;
  - H§ = handoff;
  - mem: = a memory note.
- A later record of a fix closes a row. Where the fix was partial, only the remainder is carried. Closed examples:
  - M19-233 by E2/E3;
  - M19-170 by E43/E79;
  - BRF-58 by E122.
- Status is as recorded, with its date. Where the newest source is older than 10 November it reads "unverified since
  <date>". Rows of the 9 November reviews and of the M19 closing audit (M19-239..283) are dated 10 November, the
  closure.
- Home is one of:
  - **M20 close**: only small first-hour defects, plus M20's own named gates;
  - **M21**;
  - **later**.

Round 5: the den's rumour goes stale once the beasts are dead, driven or laid (data/situations/den, Rule word_old). Round 6: the innkeeper's site topic, asked once, had its "Ask … again about" copy (WorldConstructs.recall) replay the kind's saying and the way after the den was dealt with ("There is always something living in it"; reproduced in thorn, w11 Lonely Hole). Decided by the doctrine (what the world has moved past is not told as standing): the copy is hearsay, and once the company has been there it has seen past it, whatever the scenario on the site; so RegionSites writes the site topic once only and its copy itself, offered until been(party, site), not knowing the scenario (no cross-module condition needed). ## Owner directives possibly unmet

> **Superseded (12 November, early):** this list dates from 11 November. Take the current account, brief by brief, from `mission-20/briefs-audit.md`; its omissions are §21 here (R10-01..06). The list is kept below unchanged for the record.

From the memory notes and the log's amendments. "Possibly" means no source records them done; this is not proof
they were not done.

1. **Human acceptance.** PD-14's five cold players, asked since M19, have not been run, and round 7 on the deployed
   build, required before closure (11 Nov evening), has not been run either. Owner: "M20 not closed because the
   defect list is shorter" (FH-01, FH-02).
2. **Review the two branches carefully, integrate what verifies, document the deferred conflicts** (m20-owner-before-closure
   §5). These are `adventure/fs-integrate` and `claude/great-hamilton-xr4iyo`. All that exists is a scratch commit,
   `7386d608` on `review/fs-integrate` ("not for main"). Neither branch is an ancestor of HEAD, and no document
   records the outcome.
3. **The map's panel hierarchy**, including "Explore nearby only where real" (m20-owner-map-panel-hex-kits). It is
   first in the log's open queue, and no row closes it (NM-02).
4. **Map labels at the edge must stay selectable**, never faded into inaccessibility (m20-owner-before-closure §2).
   E130 keeps them inside the safe zone and selectable; their crowding on a phone is still open (NM-03).
5. **Amendment 8:**
   - the silver walker's danger scarcity written up (frequency against balance);
   - bounded first-hour danger;
   - walkers kept as a recurring, recorded diagnostic over several characters and seeds.

   No write-up is found (CB-08, TC-07).
6. **Amendment 7:**
   - (3) a cheap public-action gauntlet;
   - (4) consolidation: overlapping tests, obsolete UI paths, the verification loop measured, stale documents;
   - (5) the authoring trail is only "page 10 done";
   - (2) creation's skills, background and spells made clear and useful.

   None is recorded as done (TC-06, TC-07, AU-01, FH-12).
7. **CI shards rebalanced** (shard 4 at 45 minutes) is in the open queue and also in the opening brief, and has not
   been done (TC-01).
8. **Atlas synthesis, "M20 now" item 5:** verify the existing character-specific perception in real play. There is
   no record (AP-12).
9. **Acceptance defects B6:** replay through both the headless projection and the rendered screen, narrow viewports
   included, checking that text, objective focus, map signals and next choices agree. The pieces exist
   (client-parity, safe-zone-browser), but no combined replay is recorded.
10. **User notes of 10 November:**
    - "remove Map from the top rail". E70 kept the rail's Map deliberately (cold players leaned on it), and E104 moved
      the rail to the foot. This is a builder decision against the literal ask, not yet confirmed by the owner.
    - "Travelling absolutely has to reveal the landscape you're passing through", and "a chance of encounter at every
      hex". E70 covers pace and rations only; the per-hex encounter is a deferred design opportunity (NM-06).
11. **Directives from M19 deferred into M20 with no M20 record:**
    - Notes and Story split (M19-228) and POV-bound narration (M19-229): m19-owner-stabilization-nov9;
    - talk as 3–4 salient topics with progression (M19-227): m19-owner-interaction-flow;
    - one metropolis composed by several adventures (M19-77/107): m19-owner-experience-pass;
    - OSR mechanics beyond breath (M19-81): m19-owner-osr-mechanics;
    - a two-company preset-party proof (M19-75): m19-owner-preset-party;
    - typed ModuleShadow (M19-69) and PD-08: m19-owner-architecture-review;
    - unified design foundations (M19-231): m19-owner-opening-and-foundations;
    - journeys A–D rendered (M19-173);
    - Common names from a true common tongue (OWN-41): m18-owner-portraits-names-2.

    - authored-country hex rule decided country by country;
    - the knowledge-boundary test (M19-272).

    The other first-hour blockers it named (M19-233 plunder, M19-170 light) are closed by E2/E3 and E43/E79.

## 26. The owner's continuity report (14 November)

A human play attempt broke down quickly. The owner's words are in `mission-21/owner-continuity.md`; the plan for them
is `mission-21/continuity-plan.md`. These are existing M21 obligations (one game-native interaction system), not new
features. None is closed as a component migration until the whole ordinary action path is audited and shown in play.

## 28. The owner's planar grand tour (14 November, night)

The owner: a mage with Plane Shift goes to each inner and outer plane, and the specs assert each plane's laws read and act differently, as low-order as the architecture allows, with lazy generation and determinism in mind (travel to the Plane of Fire and take fire damage each turn; every elemental interaction present and resistible, blockable or wardable). Given to the rules line after the descent repair (completion plan §4).

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GT-01 | Every plane differs in play: an inner plane's law has an ambient effect by its element, per round in a fight and per watch out of one, through the ordinary damage pipeline (Fire burns; Water drowns without water-breathing; the Negative drains, stopped by death ward; the Positive heals, with an overload; Earth to be judged); the outer planes keep their spell DC and gain whatever SRD trait is cheap to make real. Today a law is read only in combat and is ±1 spell DC; Earth, Water, Positive and Negative have none | partly (17 Nov): every inner plane has a law, and a law's onTurnEnd runs at the end of each turn in a fight (PlaceLaws) and each watch out of one on the company (PlaneLaws), through the damage pipeline: the Fire 1 fire (fireproof stops it), the Water 1 cold (waterproof), the Negative 1 necrotic (protected, darkproof), the Positive heals 1; the Earth judged (no harm, +1 DC), the Air no harm. The Water's ward is in the hand catalogue (17 Nov): the cold ward technique (data/catalog/abilities.json, waterproof on the world clock for two hours) granted by the sealskin ward focus (items/wonders.json), as the fire ward by the copper focus; waterproof persists past a fight as fireproof does (conditions.json); ledgered (status:waterproof, play-validated). test/small/planar-laws, run: pass. No world places the sealskin focus yet (an author's Item … is cold_ward_focus, or a seller). Death Ward (17 Nov, later, srd-proving-cases.md case 8): the SRD's wards, necrotic harm wholly turned and no level drained (a new `drain` save kind: an enervate at the warded is told as an immunity), so the Negative's law does nothing to the warded; Orsino's +5 death save kept; test/death-ward (2, mutation: the old ward fails both), test/small/planar-laws (the Negative warded). Its spell is unchanged (second level, three turns), so it wards a fight, not a watch. Not done: drowning (no breath rule), the Positive's overload, the outer planes' alignment traits (no Westernesse rule for alignment), Death Ward lasting a watch (its spell's own slice: its level moves every Life cleric's draws) | P1 | M21, rules |
| GT-02 | Plane Shift as an executable effect to a named plane's canonical threshold (today `unsupported`: effect:planeshift) | fixed (17 Nov): Plane Shift is `Travel to plane.` (hand entry over Orsino's); the canon's act "Have {caster} cast Plane Shift to {plane}" resolves invoke(plane_shift, caster, threshold) to the plane's thresholdOf; FieldUse.shift spends the slot, PlaneLaws.carry consumes the kernel's travel event and sets the company there. test/small/planar-laws, run: pass. Adapted: Imbar has no threshold, so no shift home | P1 | M21, rules |

Since (17 Nov, later): the Abyss (−1 spell DC), Nirvana (+1) and the Mount of the Exalted (+1) under laws of their own (data/catalog/conditions.json plane_abyss/nirvana/exalted, Kyr's; data/imbar/outer.kyr), and the spec holds every one of the twelve outer planes under a law (mutation: the Abyss's law taken out fails it). Not done: the integration case through a generated world and the House of Doors | P1 | M21, rules |

## 31. The owner on a place worth returning to, and earned instruments (14 November, night)

The owner's words are in `mission-21/owner-returning-instruments.md`: knowing more about the world gives the player more
interesting ways to inhabit it. One bounded experiment across the lines: the playability line the place and its walks,
the lead the engine's instrument projection, the aesthetic line the page's treatment.

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RI-01 | One existing place worth returning to: repeatable, time-consuming investigation with context-dependent discovery and encounter tables; later visits reflecting what is established, time passed, circumstance and what the character knows or can do; durable discoveries, no resettable treasure, farming or generic repeatable quests; reachable and revisitable by reliable navigation | open · From AP-17: 70 of 80 stretches round Thornhaven hold nothing; meetings sparse away from the start; half an hour without a quest finds nothing; POI density never measured (rovers improved it) · **Measured 17 Nov (round 7)**, the POI density AP-17 said was never measured (scratch /var/tmp/kyrie/m22/r7/poi.ts, a world as DrawnWorld starts it, wanderer): of the open country's stretches, those with no one and nothing at them, no sight on a condition, and no way off to a place: a1 99 of 218 (45 %), kite 104 of 235 (44 %), thorn 127 of 260 (49 %); someone or something at 31/37/43, a conditional sight at 32/48/35. A founding's scene and life lines (data/generation/scenes/foundings.json) are fixed atmosphere per kind of town, naming a fish-stall, a cheese-seller and an eel-crier who are never placed, while the town's own trades, sellers and people go unnamed in it. The rule: a place's description names only what the place holds (its works, sellers, keepers, people there now) or pure sense (light, smell, weather, sound), and draws its life from those; a concrete thing said to be there is there to be approached. A lint over drawn countries holds it, as the inscriptions' does · Partly (the lead): the market's scene and life now draw on its own trades (the baker's loaves, the brewer's ale, the carter, the tinker's pan), each a light sentence; test/small/foundings-own-trades holds every founding to naming no trade on offer but its works (the old market fails it four times). Still open: naming the people actually placed in that town, and a lint over drawn countries for any concrete thing said and never placed · **The lint over a drawn country (the lead, 15 Nov night):** test/named-held arrives at every place of a1's first town as the terminal says it (76 places with prose) and fails a tradesman, stall or keeper named and not there among its people, things or ways; mutation-checked. Its first find, in every country: the drill-yard sold "the yard's smith's best work" and no smith was ever placed (teachers.kyrt), now only the blades on the rack. Across a1, b2 and c3 (223 places) nothing else is named and not there by that lexicon. Still open: the lexicon is of trades; a concrete thing (a well, a bench, a dog) said and never placed wants the things' own names | partly (19 Nov, audit): every founding names no trade but its own works (test/small/foundings-own-trades); test/named-held fails a tradesman, stall or keeper named and not placed across a1's first town (76 places), nothing else found over a1, b2, c3 (223 places). Open: the lexicon is of trades: a concrete thing (a well, a bench, a dog) said and never placed wants the things' own names in named-held; the people actually placed in a town named in its life lines | P1 | M21, lead |
| CO-18 | Gardens are chosen by habitat alone, so a northern fishing town grows bamboo, cinnamon and amaranth (the prose editor, 15 Nov). What grows where answers to the country's climate (its latitude, its temperature) as well as to the habitat (RegionGrowth). **Fixed:** a plant may name its `climates` (RegionWeather.climate's own, validated by the canon); bamboo, cinnamon and amaranth name the warm ones, and RegionGrowth.grown leaves out a plant whose climates do not include the place's. test/small/gardens-by-climate, mutation-checked | fixed | P1 | M21, lead |
| CO-13 | The paintings' seen words are too thin for the fit rules to read: 37 of 245 scene paintings say anything of light, season or weather (scene-temple said nothing of its sun, flowering urns and cypresses, and so stood for the shrine of Danu under snow). Each scene painting looked at and given its light, season and weather · **Fixed** (paint-1, 15 Nov): all 245 looked at (contact sheets, the doubtful ones large) and given `light` (all 245: sunlit 91, lamplit 55, daylight 43, dusk 25, dim 21, firelit 20, candlelit 19, bright day 7, night 6, moonlit 2; an interior lit by a window and a fire says both), `season` only where shown (33: summer 29, autumn 2, spring, winter) and `weather` only where shown (47: clear 43, fog, rain, snow, storm) in data/paintings/seen.json; Fits reads them beside `shows` (Fits.words), and the winter rule also refuses spring and autumn. Scene paintings each rule refuses, before → after: ruined 18 → 91, foul weather 12 → 103, winter 4 → 33, evening or night 8 → 101, under the ground 25 → 99. The cost, by the owner's rule (a dim ground before a picture against the words): of the 147 kind lists in data/paintings/scenery.json, those left with no painting that fits rise from 7 to 50 in snow, 6 to 50 in rain, 5 to 49 by night, 12 to 44 under the ground. Held by test/small/paintings-season (the temple is not the snowy shrine's, a snowbound town and a lamplit shrine still are; a summer garden in snow, a sunny market by night, a bright bailey in rain, a sunlit archive under the ground are refused only by what they were looked at for), mutation-checked: reading `shows` alone fails it. Not judged: the portals of the House of Doors are given the hall's lamplight, not their worlds' weather; mist in a valley below is not weather; the registry (data/paintings/registry.json) is rebuilt only by scripts/paintings.ts, which passes the fields through | fixed | P1 | M21, an agent with eyes on the images |

Peahgiba, a name of another tongue in a human land, no longer comes: the heartland's land is named in Common (LandNames; a1, kite, iscoed, thorn and ember checked); a dwarf country's land is Khuzdul, as it should be. | AX-03 | The shared rules are recomposed and revalidated by each of 39 installs, 15–20 ms each: compile once per edition, compose by reference · **Measured (15 Nov night, a1 started as a ranger, Costs and `bun --cpu-prof-md`):** the 39 installs are 2.25 s of a 5.5 s start (41 %), about 58 ms each; within them ModuleCompiler.validate 0.68 s and its check 0.46 s, ModuleInstallations.check 0.31 s, the bundle's loading 1.0 s (its ohm semantics the largest share of the whole start). Each install validates the whole composed module again, the shared rules' every action, rule and query. A cache of declarations already validated is sound only if validity is monotone in the module it is checked against (a shared declaration first checked beside one module's relations must hold without them): prove that first, then validate by reference | partly · **18 Nov (compile-1, compile-once-plan.md steps 2–3):** once per edition is unsound, test/shared-validity: a shared declaration's validity depends on the module beside it both ways (a place named `site` refuses Lay to rest; alone the shared rules name a pole only composition declares). ## 36. Empty words (the owner and the prose-density audit, 14 November)

Scope (the owner's brief), not defects found.

The audit is `history/reviews/prose-density-audit-2026-11-13.md`. The owner adds that empty words are an obstacle:
they make the game feel slower and more pointless. The long, detailed compound sentence is the engine's worst tic, and
a three-sentence budget does not catch it. Less text also helps performance at the limit.
- The measure is `src/presentation/clause-load.ts`. A heavy sentence passes 22 words or 3 joints.
- The terminal walks report the clause load of everything said unasked.
- `bun scripts/clause-census.ts [--first-hour]` is the editors' worklist.
- `test/small/clause-load` is the ratchet. It holds the first hour's heavy sentences at 1,256 of 7,275 (17.3 %). The
  whole authored corpus stands at 20.1 %.

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## 40. Purposeful exploration (the owner, 15 November)

The generated-world walks' 292 unpursuable turns are mostly walkers inside the very site their aim names, seeing only
"explore". Gold stays honest. The failure is that exploration lacks purpose. These are defects found, not new scope.

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| EX-01 | Sample the 292 turns and class them: navigational confusion, repeated rooms, unreachable aim content, missing investigation acts, genuine lack of interesting content. This is the evidence before any change · Done (playability line): 241 lost turns classed in terminal/experience.md (85 unreachable aim content, 81 navigational confusion, 35 repeated rooms, 35 no content toward the aim, 5 missing investigation acts) by scripts/exploration-sample.ts | done | P1 | M21, playability |
| EX-02 | Three things told apart in the authoritative projection: known progress on the undertaking (an evidenced next step, gold's alone); the unexplored frontier (a reachable way or line of investigation whose contents are unknown); the known retreat or return (a way back to familiar ground). The frontier is a small directional sign or a contextual word, never a new global colour, and never a claim that the objective lies that way. "Further in" means an unexplored frontier, lateral as well as deeper · Done: Offered.bearing (frontier, retreat, return) in a site or under the ground, typed for every client (Offers.borne); retreats and ways to known ground never held (f5cd97de); held acts say their aim (Promoted.aim). Drawn (play-18): the terminal's row says "unexplored" or "back" (text only); the page's way row takes the direction sign (in, out) and the same word, both from Signs.bearing by the way's command (test/small/bearing-signs) | done | P1 | M21, playability, lead (review) |
| EX-03 | The one exception: an aim that asks to explore or survey an area makes exploring it gold progress; an aim to find an unknown thing within an area does not, without evidence · Not begun: no aim in the data yet asks to explore or survey an area as such | fixed (17 Nov, round 7): decided that an aim asks to explore or survey a place by waiting on having been in its rooms (`when not (been(party, well) and been(party, store))`, as each work's _sought aim waits on its heart room); such an aim's gold now goes room by room, from a side room back through the hall, until it is done (Glance.sought reads AimAdvances.unreached by the known ways; AimLead `beyond`, and within a site it is no longer led back out to the place the aim names). An aim to find an unknown thing within an area still gilds nothing there without evidence (AimAdvances.sought: what is hidden, where it is found). test/small/aim-lead ("goes room by room…", fails without), gold*, aim-*, nav-gold, gold-walk/follow/thread, traces, matrix-walk, page-follower: pass · was: open | P1 | M21, playability |
| EX-04 | A small regression: a walker entering the aim's site tells unvisited ways from the retreat, pursues an investigation, meets a meaningful clue or obstacle, and makes recognisable progress, with no false certainty · Done: test/small/frontier (the laboratory walked straight: frontier and retreat told apart, no exploring retreat, an aim seen through) | done | P1 | M21, playability |
| EX-05 | A site's opener the country does not supply: "needs brawn" (Ironkeep's portcullis, Tinhold's lift-cage) has no seller, and nothing reads the hire and recruit data for someone of brawn who could come. The barred way's need is said, and its means (Means) where the world has them: a coil of rope at Mik's for Ironkeep's breach. For brawn, a world-generation gap | partly (19 Nov): the galleries' lift-cage wheel needing brawn or crowbar (data/generation/works/galleries.kyrt:47), as deep-mechanisms already wrote it; the great barrow's seal-stone and the roadstation's gate still brawn alone | P2 | M21, playability, generation |


## 41. Experiential pins, by family (the owner, 15 November)

Recurring complaints become a small, durable suite of cheap behavioural pins, organised by family: promise fidelity, attention economy, continuity of intention, opportunity density, character differentiation, computation locality. Each runs complaint → trace → minimal test → root-cause repair → regression. The family map, its pins, baselines and gaps are in [mission-21/experiential-pins.md](mission-21/experiential-pins.md). Rows here are the defects and gaps the pins found.

The rows here carry two of the six families; the rest are carried by DN-01..05 (attention), EX-01..05 and PB-16 (continuity of intention), BO-01..10 (opportunity density), RI-03 and the feature pins (character differentiation) (the audit, 17 November).

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF-01 | A drawn facet is a promise its act keeps: test/small/promises-kept (each facet's act done in a fresh world, its changes read by relation; mutation-checked) and scripts/promise-fidelity.ts over curious walks on a1/b2/c3. Baseline after PF-02: knowledge 15/15, 8/8, 8/8; companionship 3/3, 3/3; worth a look 9/15, 11/15, 11/15 | fixed | P1 | M21, lead |
| PF-02 | The runner (the terminal's agents, the walkers) offered an opener done by its first act under the opener's words: "To the old corn-store — look at the corn-store padlock" pushed at the padlock. **Fixed:** Runner.opener calls it by the act it does ("Push and pull at the corn-store padlock"). test/small/runner-opener, mutation-checked | fixed | P1 | M21, lead |
| PF-03 | Every remaining break is the `held` why (an aim heard of, not followed → worth a look). Inside a manor, "To the forecourt of … Hall", a way back out to a visited place, is drawn silver from every room (b2, c3); Eogan's topic about the acolyte changes nothing typed (a1). Ruling: `held` never marks a way that leaves a site or returns to a known place (that is retreat, EX-02); on a topic only where it gives something typed toward that aim. **Gap:** a Promoted with why `held` does not say which aim it is for, so its progress cannot be checked **Fixed (the playability line, play-15/16, on main 75cebc64):** `held` dropped from any retreat and any way to a visited place (Affordances.way, test/small/held-ways); a held topic kept only where it teaches what a held aim waits on or leads to its places (Undertakings.serving); the held aim's id on the act (Promoted.aim). The laboratory's thirty-turn fidelity walk now keeps every facet drawn (knowledge 13/13, worth a look 20/20, danger 2/2, one check judged apart): test/laboratory-promises, which failed on the held topic before play-16 | fixed | P1 | M21, playability |
| LC-01 | `canTrain` is written, once a member has 20 experience, for every declared ability they have not learned (a dragon's breaths included): a world-sized fact set per member, and noise in every event's changes. Play is unaffected (`teaches` gates each lesson); narrowing it needs care, as `teaches` is asserted by rules mid-play | open (19 Nov, re-checked): canTrain is still written for every declared ability not learned once a member has 20 experience; the runner leaves canTrain out of a turn's changes as noise (RunnerPayoff.kept, 8b62edf97), which hides it without narrowing it | P3 | M21, lead |
| LC-02 | The first observation of an unchanged cellar still grows with the world (63 → 220 ms from 0 to 4,000 rooms elsewhere): it builds the module's Operators. Looking again keeps a long tail of per-entity scans (loading-boundaries.md) · **Profiled (15 Nov night, `bun --cpu-prof-md`, 8,000 rooms: start 6.2 s, first observe 321 ms, again 113 ms):** no frame of the observation exceeds about 21 ms (Operators' bindings 21, the screen 19, CompanyRecall 16, VisitedPlaces 8): a long tail, no single repair. The process's largest cost is `structuredClone`, 1.5 s, in start, save and restore (the Campaign's copy of a whole world, `fork`), which copy the world by design and are pinned as linear by test/working-set | open (19 Nov, re-checked): profiled (15 Nov night), a long tail with no frame over about 21 ms; no repair since. structuredClone is the next lever (compile-once-plan.md step 3, 892ef0af8, copies only where the reader changes) | P2 | M21, lead |
| LC-03 | Waiting costs about 7 s a game day on a drawn country (silent-lord-malice's nine-day wait: 65 s of its 136 s; the slowest spec in the rest lane, 19 kept CI-minutes): hour by hour, each leg builds a whole screen (Journey.perform, 44 % of a three-day wait's profile) and the world's watches run (18 %). The map's ways are kept per world object, so every hour reads them again. **Measured, not fixed (15 Nov night):** a trace of what the chart reads (32 relation names, never the clock) shows its reads unchanged in only 7 of 27 hours, because `at` changes most hours as someone somewhere moves: stamps per relation name (World.stamped, agrees) are too coarse for a living country. **Gap:** stamps by relation and subject (at(party) apart from at(a sentry far off)), or views kept by the places they concern, before views can be kept across a wait; until then a long wait in a spec or a night's sleep pays a screen per hour | open (19 Nov, re-checked): measured, not fixed; the gap stands: stamps by relation and subject, or views kept by the places they concern, so that an hour's screen need not be built again in a living country | P2 | M21, lead |
| LC-04 | The rest lane made cheaper (the fast line, 15 Nov). **Kept times refreshed** from the full run 43577544153 (scripts/timings-from-ci.ts --all): the rest lane was 680 kept CI-minutes on times kept before the night's "-alone" twins, 503 measured. **A wait told only as it ends** (Journey's `==`, opt-in): the hours between are played by the wait the page last offered, through the session and its rules, their pages not built; three days' wait in kahitude 19.1 s to 9.9 s, the digest after every step the same, what each hour told the same (test/small/quiet-wait, mutation-checked: a quiet hour that loses what it told, or a last hour left unnarrated, fails it). Used only by specs that read the world after a wait, never its pages. **Shared making** (Kept.of, and Kept.drawn in the howe's, the keep's, the House's and the ties' helpers): a prefix every case played again is made once in a file and resumed. Measured locally: the Slack 424 s to 141 s; the King's Players' acts, standing-modules-found, Act IV, the circus journeys, walk-ends and walk-danger as in the fast line's report; new CI times wait for the next full run. **Gap (engine):** what is left in a drawn country is the step itself, about 0.5 to 0.7 s (a screen 0.5 s, a while 0.7 s; a resume 0.7 s for thorn), and in a quiet hour the world's watches (60 %), and PlaySession.leg asking PlayerView.choices for every wait only to learn whether it crosses a hex (whither, 24 %) | partly (19 Nov, re-checked): a wait told only as it ends (Journey's ==, df88eb867) and kept times refreshed; CI lanes reworked since (15 Nov: the push lane under thirty kept seconds a file, the whole nightly). Remains: the engine's step in a drawn country, 0.5–0.7 s, and PlaySession.leg asking PlayerView.choices for every wait (OR-91, AX-05) | P2 | M21, fast |

## 42. The second blind playtest (the lead's Gate 1, tier 3; 15 November)

A blind agent played the deployed build (2e603c8a) as a first-time player, two runs in two countries, judging only the page (history/playtests/blind-agent-2026-11-15, with screenshots). No crash, hang or console error. It would play again tomorrow; what would stop it is "sameness and small untruths, not friction". Fixed since the 13 November playtest by its own account: holding to choose in creation, the map not routing to the goal, six-round foregone fights, rules headings. ## 44. The owner's headless pull review (14 November)

From one terminal play (doc/reviews/headless-pull-review-2026-11-14.md, the owner's): seed `astonishment-review`, Rowan a human ranger, wanderer. The run went through the causeway wagoner, Motosta, Kava's corn-store, a candle and the rat-runs. The judgment: possibility arrives faster than attachment. The next proof is one expedition that changes the meaning of a person, an object and a home, not more breadth. Each finding is kept with its acceptance as the review gives it.

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HP-01 | Opportunity glut before desire: one notice and one question left four held aims (Icelake's reading-glass, Kava's rats, Jassin's churchyard, Baeloth's quarry) among 105 acts. **Wants:** a notice gives leads, not held undertakings. A lead becomes an aim on speaking to its owner, witnessing its consequence, or a deliberate keep. **North star (the owner's test):** after one hour, can the player name a place they regard as home, one image they mean to return to, one person whose view matters to them, and one consequence whose meaning remains contested?

## 45. The owner's play, 15 November (afternoon): a mage cannot cast

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OP-05 | **P0.** "I created a mage, entered combat with rats, and could not successfully target enemies with Magic Missile or target myself for applicable buffs. I died while trying to use basic spells." **Reproduced** in the page (a1, the calling Mage chosen at creation, its school the warding one by default, the corn-store's rats): the Spells panel offered Minor Ward, Arcane Ward and Light, and Mage Armor, Magic Missile, Magebane and Major Ward each said "no spell of its level left to cast" in the first round. **The layer:** neither targeting, presentation nor execution. Eligibility (Battle.usable → Spellcasting.affordable) bound each slot to the one spell prepared in it, and a first preparation took the first spells learned (the school's wards before Magic Missile), so two slots held Arcane Ward and Light. Every other known spell of the level was refused, with a reason that was not true. Spells above the caster's level said the same, because the slot check ran before the level check. **Fixed (15 Nov):** a spell known is cast from any ready slot of its level or higher, the lowest ready, as the SRD has it. The preparation each slot names stays for the sheet and for preparing, but no longer binds what it casts. Unusable reasons are checked level first ("beyond your level yet"), and the count of casts ready is the slots of the level or higher (CombatModel). The pinned Vancian rule (test/spellcasting) is rewritten to the new one: Resist Fire, known and unprepared, is cast from the second-level slot. **Held by** test/small/mage-casts, a mage made through CreationFlow (calling Mage, the warding school), a companion and two rats:<br>- the page's fight menu offers Magic Missile at each rat, Mage Armor on itself, and Arcane Ward on itself or the companion;<br>- each casts, with its effect on its target;<br>- the terminal casts Magic Missile by name and a rat's number.<br>All three fail with the old slot rule. **Three balance specs had been calibrated to the old, weaker caster and moved with it (full run 43622497676):** a lone mage no longer dies to the barrow's beetles (the obituary spec now fights the barrow-dead, test/mage-play); the Quittance glass men are fought by a martial company of the seventh level, not the sixth, since a cleric who can cast any spell she knows changed the dice (test/quittance-descent); and the howe's horse-hall, which killed four of the third level with a mage in them, now lets them through (they die at the first; at the second one falls: test/bound-howe-under). That last is a rules-line question: where the howe's asking level should now sit. **Verified in the real page (a1, a mage made at creation, the corn-store's rat-runs):** Mage Armor is cast at once on the mage (no target to choose; "Mage-Armored", and the rats then needed a 19 to hit); Magic Missile opens the aim with all three rats lit and one falls; Arcane Ward and Light are offered at the mage. **Also found, not mended (CB-03):** cast first, with no armour, a lone first-level mage (4–5 hit points) was killed in the first round by the two rats left. The game had reckoned that fight "more than you can take" (three rats against a lone level-1 mage, deadly); the aim's job leads a lone caster there. A forecast kept honest, not a way through: CB-03 stays open | fixed | P0 | M21, rules |

## 46. The casting-fiction invariant (the owner, 15 November)

Causal legibility: who did this, by what learned capability, at what cost, under what rules, and what happened. A calling
grants education and vocabulary, an ability produces an effect, an authored action arranges circumstances and invokes
capabilities and never invents an unnamed power. Written up with the audit in
doc/architecture/mission-21/casting-fiction.md.

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CF-01 | The invariant: every authored action that changes the world by magic resolves through a named ability, item, ritual, environmental mechanism or canonical calling technique, and its event keeps caster, means, source, targets, cost, attack/save/resistance results and effects, inspectable after reload. **Written and audited (15 Nov):** of 14 authored Slay/Restore actions (scripts/casting-audit), none invokes an ability, 4 hold and spend an item, 2 are gated on a calling alone, 8 have no named means (a rite, a spring and a battle among them, to review); 52 actions are gated on hasCalling/learned (51 assert a fact), unreviewed. Held as a ratchet that may shrink and not grow (test/small/casting-audit) | partly (19 Nov, re-checked): the audit held as a ratchet (test/small/casting-audit); bun scripts/casting-audit.ts today: 13 consequential actions, 0 invoke an ability, 4 hold and spend an item, 1 gated on a calling (undertown's brood spelled), 8 bare (sallowfield's battle and arcs, tallow's lay_dead). Remains: the 8 bare given a named means, and the 52 calling-gated actions reviewed (CF-05) | P1 | M21, rules |
| CF-02 | The undertown's `_u_rats_slept` ("a mage's charm, not the Sleep spell") and `_u_brood_spelled` ("a magician's fire"): a calling alone, free, no bound caster, no area, save, resistance or Hit Dice limit, `Slay` outright (sleep and kill conflated), a power no canon, handbook or forecast names. **Acceptance (the owner):** a mage without Sleep is not offered it; one who can cast it is named, and Sleep by its canonical name; the same cost and the same target, save, resistance and duration rules as elsewhere; caster, spell, targets and results recorded; resisters stay active; sleepers are asleep, not dead; killing them is a distinct action; save/reload keeps it; terminal, browser, forecast and handbook agree | fixed (16 Nov): `_u_rats_slept` is "Have {caster} cast Sleep over the rats in the run": canCast(caster, sleep), Resolve invoke(sleep, caster, rats) (CF-03's route: the slot, each rat's save), what resists or wakes comes at the company (assails); "Do for the sleeping rats where they lie" is a separate act (Resolve combat when not able and not dead); the job rule clears on dead, or not able and not lulled, so sleeping rats are not cleared; the laboratory's Lowmouth the same. Held by test/small/casting-audit (the calling-only debt is the brood's fire alone), the laboratory specs, test/undertown*, honest-start, engine-names. Not played in a generated first hour: Sleep is third-level in the catalogue, so no starting mage casts it · **Since (17 Nov, later, srd-proving-cases.md case 14):** Sleep is the SRD's first-level spell, so a starting mage who knows it (an enchanter one time in two, a drawn-school mage one in six) casts it at the run's rats; four Hit Dice of them, and a rat who saves comes at the company. Held by test/sleep and test/small/sleep-band | P1 | M21, rules |
| CF-03 | The shared invocation route cannot take a band: `Resolve invoke(ability, caster, target)` (care.kyr's field_spell) takes members and field-usable techniques only. Needed: a group's creatures as targets with legal-target selection, cost, attack/SR/save and effects through the one route; a projected `sleeping(group)` fact from the creature's condition; the aftermath ("Kill the sleeping rats") a separate authored action. Cheapest next step: a tiny world, a mage who knows Sleep, three rats and `Resolve invoke(sleep, caster, rats)`  **Probed (15 Nov, a tiny world):** the compiler rejects a group as `invoke`'s target (declared `(ability, member, member)`), and `FieldUse.perform` refuses any ability that is not field-safe, by design; so the route for a hostile spell is the fight. **Refined capability:** an opening move for a combat resolve (a named caster's cast as its first act), not a band as a field target; see casting-fiction.md | fixed (16 Nov, M22 proving case 1): `Resolve invoke(ability, caster, band)` takes a group (its target's kind `member` or `group`, checked by the compiler); a hostile spell that lays those it is cast at out of the fight (asleep, held) runs at the band outside a fight by the fight's own rules (FieldUse.cast: the named caster's slot, each save, immunity), the condition kept on the world's clock; sleepers are not able (the engine's `able`, no second fact), not dead, the ordinary fight not offered; killing them a separate act; resisters and wakers come at the company through `assails`; a save keeps it. Held by test/small/sleep-band (4 cases; before, the compiler refused the band: "Invalid argument"). Record: mission-22/srd-proving-cases.md. Adapted: Sleep keeps the catalogue's numbers (level 3, DC 16+level, all enemies, no Hit Dice limit) | P1 | M21, rules (engine) |
| CF-04 | Firebomb and ratsbane actions (`_u_nest_burned`, `_u_brood_burned`, `_u_spiders_burned`, `_u_rats_baited`): the item is named and spent, but the effect is a bare `Slay` and the item's own attack, save and damage never run; convert through the item's catalog effect | open (19 Nov, re-checked): bun scripts/casting-audit.ts (19 Nov) still lists the undertown's nest, brood and spiders burned as item Slay with no named effect (data/generation/town/undertown.kyrt); the rats baited is not counted at all, since its Slay is a later Rule (_u_rats_poisoned, 36 watches on), not the action | P2 | M21, rules |
| CF-05 | Review the 52 calling-gated actions and the 8 bare Slay/Restore: each answers who acts, the means, whether it is usable now, its target, cost, resolution, what persists and what is authored, or is declared a plain recognition, a rite or an environmental mechanism; then a lint | open (19 Nov, re-checked): the casting audit's 8 bare Slay/Restore actions (sallowfield, tallow) and the 52 calling-gated actions unreviewed; the ratchet holds (test/small/casting-audit) | P2 | M21, authoring |

No capability was missing. **Observation:** the author invented `deeded`; two authors would invent two. A shared ownership relation beside dominion's `holds` (a power's) would let rules, the page and the handbook read what the company owns generically: a library pattern to decide, not a gap | partly (19 Nov, re-checked): the Landholder pinned on a tiny world (test/small/landholder). Remains: a shared ownership relation beside dominion's holds, a library decision so two authors do not invent two | P2 | M21, authoring |

| PV-05 | **The Eye:** a mixed party (warrior, thief, mage) against a discerner or a dragon. **Pinned (15 Nov):** lost below the monster's level and won above it (dragon 0 / 0 / 7 / 86 % at party levels 5 / 8 / 12 / 16; discerner 64 / 100 / 100 / 100 %), the forecast agreeing and naming who could fall (test/the-eye; both directions mutation-checked). **Found:** the dragon's hit points (99) and fights do not scale with its level; a discerner at the party's level is 22–40 rounds; a cleric in place of the thief or mage lifts the win rate sharply (64 → 100 %, 7 → 71 %), a differentiation question | partly (19 Nov, audit): pinned (test/the-eye, both ways mutation-checked). | SR-01 | The SRD spell migration by proof slices (rules/proof-slices.md, step 5's elemental family: Burning Hands, Fireball, Cone of Cold): until 17 Nov only Magic Missile ran by the SRD's numbers; every other spell with a counterpart ran by Orsino's | partly (17 Nov, mission-22/srd-proving-cases.md case 7): **Cone of Cold** by the SRD's numbers (hand entry over Orsino's): fifth level, 1d6 cold a casting level to 15d6, a cone catching friend and foe, a Reflex save for half at the SRD's difficulty and half the casting level (the migration's DC conversion: a Westernesse save adds the saver's whole level), read through `#castingScore`, new in every actor's context (a calling's casting score, else Charisma). test/cone-of-cold (6; Orsino's entry put back fails four). The census grades it in structure, resolution and play identity (2 of 119 pass in resolution now). Met by a mage of the ninth level or a hostile evoker or generalist of it; in a1 and the six fidelity countries no installed creature knows it (casters stand at the third level at most), so no first hour moves. **The line and Lightning Bolt** (17 Nov, later, case 9): `line` is the third area shape (Positions: the one aimed at, and the first standing in each place crossed before and beyond, friend or foe; aimed at a foe, not a place); Lightning Bolt 1d6 electricity a casting level to 10d6 along it, a Reflex save for half at case 7's DC, its level kept at Orsino's fourth (structure fails, said); test/lightning-bolt (7; the line taken out fails three); met by a seventh-level mage or hostile evoker, the chimera, air and storm elementals and the living book, none of them installed in a1 or the six countries at the start. 3 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Stopped by the census, a decision for the lead** (case 10): Burning Hands (2 → 1), Fireball (4 → 3) and Lightning Bolt (4 → 3) at the SRD's levels would give 74–100 % of drawn mages other spells. A first-level evoker would know Fireball 22 % of the time against 0 %. Creation draws a spell by its index in a level's sorted list, so one spell more moves every later draw. The player's mage, hirelings, mage specs and journeys would move; a1, kite and thorn's 962 installed classed creatures would not. The chain is the same: every cascading spell enters a draw, and Chain Lightning alone moves 36 % of ninth-level and 77 % of twelfth-level drawn mages (the veterans' Maeve among them). The options: re-record once; decouple the draw (rank options by a hash of the seed and the spell's id, then one re-record; recommended); or keep Orsino's levels, saying so. **Option 2 chosen by the lead, built** (17 Nov, case 11): a drawn caster's spells are the least of their options ranked by a hash of its spells stream's seed and each spell's id (SpellSelection.ranked). A dummy first-level evocation now moves exactly the 18–29 % of mages who gain it, where the old draw moved 68–96 %. test/spell-draw (4; the old draw put back fails three). Re-recorded once: the wren-tower-climbed trace's digests, tend-here, march-journey, bound-howe (CB-27), bound-howe-week-people, and skeel-browser (its six fixed waits become a bounded wait for low water). The descents did not move. **Slice 4, case 12** (17 Nov, later, resumed on the owner's word to push on all fronts): Burning Hands (2 → 1, 1d4 fire a casting level to 5d4, a cone) and Fireball (4 → 3, 1d6 a casting level to 10d6, a burst), each a Reflex save for half at case 7's DC, and Lightning Bolt at the third level beside it; test/burning-hands-fireball (6). Census: of 4,000 drawn casters, evokers 64–65 % move, abjurers 17–29 %, drawn-school mages 26–34 %, bards and clerics none, every one a caster who held or now holds a moved spell or one displaced by it; of 2,232 installed classed creatures in a1 and the six countries, one (kahitude's s5 traveller). 5 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 5, case 14** (17 Nov, later): **Sleep** by the SRD's numbers: first level, a burst, four Hit Dice fewest first and none of five or more (the mechanic built: `HitDicePool`, an effect's `hitDice`, read by the fight's legal aims and the aim's row), a Will save to negate at case 7's DC, ten rounds at every casting level (converted); a blow that wounds a sleeper wakes them (asleep's onAttacked). test/sleep (6). Census: enchanters 99–100 % move (Sleep or Charm at the first level, one in two each, where Sleep had been their one third-level enchantment), evokers 10–22 %, drawn-school mages 22–31 %, bards none; six of 2,232 installed classed creatures gain it, a1's and wren's undertown necromancer among them. 6 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 6, case 15** (17 Nov, later): **the chain**, built (an effect's `chain`, src/rules/chain.ts: the one aimed at, then a leap to each of as many other living foes, once each, in the fight's order, never a friend, each its share of what the first was dealt before its save), and **Chain Lightning** by it: sixth level, 1d6 a casting level to 20d6, a leap a casting level to twenty at half, Reflex half each at case 7's DC; test/chain-lightning (4). Census: only gainers move (5.5–15.5 % of 11th–15th-level mages), no installed creature knows it. 33 abilities unsupported (34), the ratchet 96 (100). 7 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 7, case 16** (17 Nov, later): **the cure family** by the SRD's numbers: Cure Light Wounds 1d8 and a point a casting level to +5, Moderate 2d8 to +10, Serious 3d8 to +15, Critical 4d8 to +20, each conjuration beside Life; Orsino's levels kept for the three above Light (the preset companions choose by them); the harm to the undead omitted; test/cure-wounds (2). No draw moves; every cure heals more by the casting level. Before it, the SRD's "functions like" pages read whole (SrdPages.inherit: 107 records gain their header). 11 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 8, case 17** (17 Nov, later): **Bless** on the caster and every ally for ten rounds (Orsino's: one ally, three), first level, enchantment beside Life; Orsino's blessed kept (+1 hit and damage, shared by Divine Favor and Neptune's Blessing); test/bless (2). 12 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 9, case 18** (17 Nov, later): **Hold Person** by the SRD's numbers (one humanoid, Will negates at case 7's DC, five rounds) and the mechanic it needed, **shaking off** (src/rules/shaking-off.ts: a save marked `again` is made again on each turn its holder loses, a success ending it); test/hold-person (2). 13 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 10, case 19** (18 Nov): **Shield of Faith** by the SRD's numbers: first level (Orsino's second), abjuration beside Law, on the caster or one ally, two harder to hit by a deflection added to worn armour (a new condition, `shield_of_faith`, the existing `ac`), +2 at every casting level and ten rounds (converted), harmless; test/shield-of-faith (2). Census: oathkeepers 75.5 % move, other clerics 12.5–25.5 %, mages and bards none; the oathkeeper, deathspeaker and stormcaller creation cards gain it (the vitalist's does not move); six of 2,232 installed classed creatures gain it, none lose. 15 of 119 pass in resolution now (the census's count, Resurrection among them). **Slice 11, case 20** (18 Nov): **a partial save**, built (a save's `lesser`: whoever succeeds still has what it lays, for that many rounds, never the proof), and **Cause Fear** by it over Orsino's Terror: first level (Orsino's second), necromancy beside Death, one foe of five Hit Dice or fewer (case 14's pool, five on one), a fear save at case 7's DC, three rounds afraid or one for whoever saves (converted from 1d4); Orsino's fear kept, the flight and the living-only omitted; test/cause-fear (2). Census: deathspeakers 60 % move, other clerics 8.5–15.5 %, mages and bards none; the deathspeaker's creation card gains it; six of 2,232 installed classed creatures gain it, none lose; eight kinds cast it innately, none placed in a1. 16 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 12, case 21** (18 Nov): **Remove Fear** by the SRD's numbers: first level, abjuration beside Life, on the caster or one ally, the fear on them ended and +4 against fear for a hundred rounds (a new condition, `heartened`, the existing save bonus); the SRD's one more subject each four levels omitted; test/remove-fear (2). No draw or creation card moves (the vitalist's, the first cleric way, has it); five installed classed creatures know it, as before. 17 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Stopped by the census, decisions for the lead** (18 Nov): **Mage Armor** (case 22): the first mage way's (the abjurer's) card spell, Orsino's twenty temporary hit points against a first-level abjurer's 4–7 of its own, and the owner's mage-casts pin; the SRD's school would move abjurers 43 %, conjurers 23 %; options: the SRD's whole, its resolution with Orsino's school kept (recommended, after a first-hour measure), that keeping the twenty, or as it stands. **Divine Favor** (case 23): a War cantrip at Orsino's level 0, the SRD's first; every warbringer and 11–23.5 % of other clerics move, and 48 of 2,232 installed divine casters lose it (seven of a1's), none gain; options: the SRD's whole, its resolution with the cantrip kept, or as it stands. **Slice 13, case 24** (18 Nov): **Calm Emotions** by the SRD's numbers, where Orsino's calm was a boon to every foe it was cast at: second level, enchantment beside Nature, a burst on friend and foe, a Will save at case 7's DC, whoever fails calmed (a new condition, `calmed`: no actions, broken by any harm or blow, as asleep is) for three rounds (converted from the SRD's concentration); test/calm-emotions (2). No draw or creation card moves; one installed classed creature knows it, as before. 18 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 14, case 31** (18 Nov): **Entangle** by the SRD's numbers: first level, transmutation beside Nature, a burst on friend and foe (as it was), a Reflex save at case 7's DC, entangled ten rounds (converted) where Orsino's held three against 15 and the caster's level; the re-entangling each round (a persistent area), the plants it needs and the check to tear free omitted; test/entangle (2). No draw, creation card or installed creature moves; fought, a company with it and a warrior against it as before. 19 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 15, case 32** (18 Nov): **Protection from Evil** by the SRD's numbers: first level, abjuration beside Law, on the caster or one ally, warded ten rounds (a new condition, `warded_from_evil`: two harder to hit by a deflection, +2 to every save, proof against charm), against every foe (Kyr's fight asks no alignment); Orsino's three rounds of proof against charm and fear and half of necrotic harm gone; test/protection-from-evil (2). No draw, creation card or installed creature moves; a lone warrior against a warded foe 32 → 24 % at the third level. 20 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 16, case 33** (18 Nov): **Command** by the SRD's numbers: first level (Orsino's fifth), enchantment beside Law, one foe, a Will save at case 7's DC, and the SRD's "Fall": prone one round, its next turn lost, still in the fight, where Orsino's made it flee the fight against 20; test/command-fall (2). Census: only gainers move (1,545 of 19,200 drawn clerics, an oathkeeper in three), 7 of 2,233 installed classed creatures gain it and lose none; **the oathkeeper's creation card moves** (Command and Holy Wrath in place of Heat Drain and Healing Touch); fights with it come out as before. 21 of 119 pass in resolution now. **Slice 17, case 23** (18 Nov, the owner's word): **Divine Favor** by the SRD's resolution at Orsino's cantrip level: blessed alone on the caster for ten rounds, Orsino's keen gone; test/divine-favor (2). Census: only gainers and losers move (711 of 19,200 drawn mages, evokers 26.5–29.5 %), the evoker's creation card loses it at the second level, one of 2,233 installed classed creatures moves (kahitude's s5 traveller), none in a1. 24 of 119 pass in resolution now; the manifest's ready records 71 → 78. **Slice 20, case 36** (19 Nov): **Ghoul Touch** (Orsino's Paralyzing Touch) by the SRD's numbers at its own second level: one humanoid a melee touch lands on, a Fortitude save at case 7's DC, six rounds (the SRD's 1d6+2); test/ghoul-touch (3). Two variation fields built for it: `forgoes.resistances` (its base's half of every blow dropped) and `actions` (one a turn where Orsino's mummy has two). test/royal-mummy (6). Challenge 14. Only the eight keepers move; fought by companies of one to three at levels 1–3 it was lethal and stays so (the three of the third level 63 → 45 %), and fled from it moves within a few seeds (a lone first-level warrior 74 → 67 %); the golden route, by cold play 4's lines, flees and falls as it did. 3 of 72 pass in resolution now. **Stopped by the census, a decision for the lead:** the halls' five mummies (L1–L3 dwellers in kite, thorn, wren, ember, kahitude) as the SRD's become lethal (a first-level warrior and cleric 80 → 10 %); recommended: Orsino's as it stands. **Stopped by the census, case 34:** the waterworks' keeper (the still water, Orsino's water elemental at the eighth level, four worlds) as the SRD's Large makes it markedly deadlier (a lone first-level warrior gets away 85 → 48 %), as the Medium (the concordance's) markedly kinder (a first-level warrior and cleric win 5 → 55 %); recommended: Orsino's as it stands until the keepers' levels are reviewed. **Case 47** (19 Nov): the bone hound (`hound/skeletal`, the SRD's wolf skeleton) and the cave hound (the riding dog) in the concordance (74 creatures); the skeleton template's 5/bludgeoning and immunity to cold measured on the `skeletal` variation and **stopped**: iscoed's denned first-level pairs for a lone first-level warrior 93 → 57–60 %, a death 8 → 40–43 % (only the staff passes it). ## 56. Plain prose (the owner, 17 November, night)

The owner's commission: plain, exact, literal English for ordinary actions, travel, generated descriptions,
observations and consequences; remarkable moments keep remarkable sentences; acceptance is reading aloud. The policy
and the diagnosis of why recognised problems persisted are `doc/authoring/plain-prose.md`; the before and after, with
the candidate reference corpus, `doc/authoring/prose-comparison.md`. Measured by `bun scripts/prose-census.ts` (the
first hour of a1, kite, thorn and Iscoed: every origin, the road meeting's every answer, an investigating and a
wandering player).

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PP-01 | A default literary policy for ordinary events, and why the old guidance did not hold: narratology's banned table unread by anything ("with the air of a man" in every a1 walk ten days after it was banned), linters that measure density not diction (the density pass of 13 November split the carter's sentence in two and kept every figure), ornament rewarded ("name the thing" answered with invented detail), templates read unfilled, each pass polishing afresh | fixed (17 Nov): doc/authoring/plain-prose.md; narratology.md marked superseded in part, dated, its text kept | P0 | M22, prose |
| PP-02 | A prose lint: banned expressions and constructions, each earned by a cited sentence it must match (data/editorial/prose-lint.json), over every narrated string of the ordinary prose; and the faults of filled text (an unfilled slot, a word said twice, a/an, a sentence begun in lower case, a space before a stop) over the pages a first hour actually told | fixed (17 Nov): src/presentation/prose-lint.ts, scripts/prose-lint.ts; test/small/prose-lint (in check:fast) reads the sources and test/fixtures/prose/first-hour.json (re-recorded by `prose-census --fixture` when the prose moves); test/prose-filled lints a world drawn fresh, its authored modules too, for the mechanical faults | P0 | M22, prose |
| PP-03 | A watchlist of suspicious constructions for an editor, each with its evidence, failing nothing: an absolute "with" clause, a motion noun for a verb, a simile or "as if" on an ordinary act, land or weather given a mind, the narrator winking, a hedge, a body part that knows, a stock idiom | partly (19 Nov, audit): the watchlist built (data/editorial/watchlist.json, `bun scripts/prose-lint.ts --watch`). ## 57. Trust contracts: travel (the owner, 18 November)

The owner's trust contracts (mission-22/trust-contracts-brief.md), sections E, G and F; the investigation, the census
(`bun scripts/travel-census.ts`) and what was fixed are under Travel in `mission-22/trust-contracts.md`; held by
`test/small/travel-continuity.test.ts`. (The brief's TC ids were taken by the test architecture's rows.)

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TR-01 | Travel as an authoritative sequence of perceptible steps (E): each leg a real move, its time, its knowledge and an interruption point; the page showing it as it is made, never a jump the walk hides | partly (18 Nov): the engine's half holds and is pinned per leg (location, minutes, knowledge, a hurt on leg 3 stops it at leg 3; nothing rolled up front). Open: the page walks the whole journey after it is made, over the map as it was before setting out, and learns the land at once at the end (a march-stone crossed: no walk, a jump); streaming legs was tried and taken back, the board being remade on every change of land (kite's walk outlasted test/board-browser); a road leg goes all its hexes at once, the company `enRoute` and meetable nowhere for hours (iscoed: seven hexes, 230 minutes, one leg; 130 of 204 hexes travelled in the census went in legs of more than one); and a road place, once visited, has the map know its whole length and the land beside it (a1: hexes three beyond where the company left the road). Both pinned as failing. 18 Nov: what each leg brought is in the journey's telling, from the engine (`Journeyed.legs[].told`, `Travel.way`): a light run out, the world's words said in passing (a pedlar far off), water gone round, one line each in order; the runner and the terminal had told the last leg alone, so a Light lapsed on the second of three legs (CL-38) and every `passed` were told to no one, and the page's legs lacked the water (test/journey-brought) | P1 | M22, lead |
| TR-02 | Meaningless interruptions (G): routine events stopped auto-progressing journeys | partly (18 Nov): 24 stops in 28 journeys of six to eight hexes became 7 (open country 1.4 a journey to 0.36); a word said or a thing going on where a leg comes stops it only where something there asks the player (`Travel.weighty`, the glance's aspects), else it is told with the leg; a beast at home with no temper (a butterfly, a fox, a hedgehog) is no danger. Water found ahead with a way round it known is told with the leg ("the way on goes round it") and the journey re-plans and goes on; it stops only when no way round is known: 7 stops became 1 (test/small/crow-route). Open: a thing told in passing leaves no mark on the map to come back to (18 Nov: now told to the player, TR-01; not marked, since what is passed is the world's words with no place of their own: a pedlar, a coach at a ford; a place they teach is marked heard already, and a mark of anything else wants a place to tie it to and the atlas study's ruling) | P1 | M22, lead |
| TR-03 | Travel cost (F): a six-to-eight-hex journey differs from one hex only in time | open (18 Nov): measured, no number changed: 4 to 7 hours against 30 to 70 minutes; no ration eaten, nobody weary or hungry in 28 journeys; a road no faster than the land it crosses (44 minutes a hex against 34 over fields); the pack mule only carries; no horse, cart or riding beast exists. Five proposals in trust-contracts.md (roads quicker, rations by the march, fatigue by the march, beasts as tradeoffs, a reckoning before setting out): the owner's to choose | P2 | M22, owner |

## 58. Stories, not situations (the owner, 18 November: the structural and literary critique)

The owner's critique of the standing scenarios (doc/architecture/reviews/scenario-structural-literary-critique-2026-11-18.md): "Kyrie often confuses a situation with a story, a task chain with an arc, branching
solutions with dramatic choice, and persistence with consequence." Its six tests (vector, revision, pressure, agency,
collision, aftermath) and its revision protocol (the seven sentences, the scene budget, the branch and clock tests) are
the measure. The golden route's sequence comes first (Sprint 2: ST-01..ST-04).

| Id | What | Status | Pri | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ST-01 | The golden route's four scenarios (held drove, sea-gate, cold pans, Tower) each given the critique's seven-sentence structural sheet (apparent story, moving actor, opposing claim, turn, crisis, choice, aftermath) before any prose is touched | fixed (19 Nov, burn-6): all four sheets in mission-22/story-sheets.md. The held drove and the sea-gate (18 Nov); the cold pans (a deadline and no claim: its opposing claim is already on the route, the eel-men's sluice-cut the fuel boat must come down, with the rakers' arrears and a second salter's pans for a cost); the Tower reclassified as a civic institution, not a story (ST-05), with what its reckoning of the chain wants (a tally that carries what the reeve wrote across a march) | P1 | M22, lead |
| ST-02 | The held drove as adjudication with a cost: Mardle's legitimate fear and an actor clock (warning, deterioration, crisis, not only the fourth day's sending north); the evidence shown in a collision scene (Mardle, the reeve, the customs-man present); a correct ruling that costs someone something | fixed (19 Nov, burn-6): the collision. What was found is said before the reeve on the green, with Mardle called out onto the customs-house step and the customs-man beside him (Call Mardle out to the reeve, and say what you found before her): with the drowned four only, the customs-man will not look at the company and the reeve rules for Bran and leaves Mardle his book (the landing: he will count the next drove straight, with her looking); with the ink, the customs-man takes the company's side (he counted thirty-six at the march-stone with Mardle), Mardle's fear is said aloud and he is turned out, his family with him. She is not told it again as news (the gate's reeve_mardle reads drove.reeve_ruled). test/drove-reeve.
